### Fragments: August 18
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-08-18.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Part of the reason why I’m at Thoughtworks is because I’d like to see a software development organization founded on technical excellence as an example for the rest of the industry. The trouble is that I have little aptitude or inclination for the hard work of building such an organization. So I rely on working with people who are prepared to actually put the effort in. A key partner in all of this is Rachel Laycock , who is the global CTO of Thoughtworks. Not just is she far better than me at running a technology organization, she’s also a keen observer and connector of ideas. I’ve been urging her to write these down, even if her busy schedule makes it difficult for her to compose them into something substantial. Happily she’s starting writing “Rachel’s Ramblings” Fast, imperfect, thinking out loud. Naming ideas early rather than waiting until they’re fully formed. Because the reality is, most of what I do day to day isn’t answering known questions. It’s spotting patterns and asking questions we haven’t quite figured out yet.

### Citizens Build, Agents Execute, Experts Govern
Source: Martin Fowler
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings/citizens-agents-experts.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

TL;DR Why building an app over the weekend isn't the same as building enterprise software I’ve noticed an interesting gap opening up over the last six months. It isn’t really a gap in technology. It’s a gap in what different people think software engineering actually is. The conversation usually starts the same way. A non-techie, maybe an executive, tells me about something they’ve built over the weekend. Sometimes it’s a chatbot. Sometimes it’s an internal workflow. Sometimes it’s a surprisingly polished application that solves a real business problem. They’re excited, and they should be. Twelve months ago they probably couldn’t have built it at all. Then comes the question. “If AI can do this now, why aren’t our engineering teams delivering ten times faster?” It’s a perfectly reasonable question, after all we’ve all seen the demos. The first thing that would come to my head is “you don’t know what it takes to build enterprise grade software”. But then I think about what I mean and how to explain it to a non-technical person without sounding super patronising.

### 333 Tor Exit Nodes Hijacked To Intercept SSH Traffic And Retrieve Plaintext Email
Source: SoylentNews
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://soylentnews.org/breakingnews/article.pl?sid=26/10/22/1248234&from=rss
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

https://arstechnica.com/information-technology/2026/10/300-tor-exit-nodes-hijacked-to-intercept-ssh-traffic-and-retrieve-plaintext-email/ Unknown hackers hijacked more than 300 Tor exit nodes and reconfigured them to favor unencrypted email traffic and redirect SSH connections through servers they controlled, a researcher reported Tuesday. The hijacking began in late August, when according to Tor volunteers , the attacker used a valid password that had been reused to gain access to a Tor management server. The attacker then installed Ebury , a backdoor written for Linux that was discovered 12 years ago. Then the attacker connected from the management server to a second Tor server, called Switzerland2, through SSH, using an RSA key that, for unknown reasons, investigators couldn't find among Switzerland2's authorized keys. With administrative control of Switzerland2, the attackers installed Ebury there as well. Finally, the attacker reconfigured settings on two exit nodes that Switzerland2 hosted.

Tool: Tree Appreciator Another game experiment with Claude. This one used the default Claude iPhone app (chat mode, not Claude Code mode), where I prompted Claude Opus 5.5 Max with: Build a three js game called "Tree appreciator" - it shows you two algorithmically generated trees and the game mechanics is about appreciating the differences between them. Think hard about how to make sure it is both fun and beautiful. Somewhat embarrassingly given my recent talk , I've found that telling Opus 5.5 "make sure it is fun" works uncomfortably well. ### LaTeX renderer
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/15/latex/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Tool: LaTeX renderer Inspired by Paul Ford and Aboard Foundary's new web app Tiny Little Office , which provides a suite of web app content editors (a Word Processor, Outliner, Presentation Builder, Spreadsheet, Calculator, Drawing Tool, Diagram Maker, Animator, Calendar, and Trip Planner). Tiny Little Office's signature trick is to store all of the document data in the #fragment section of the URL, so every document created using it becomes a bookmarkable and shareable link. It also has an llms.txt file telling agents how to construct URLs that will render as documents. Claude used that file to generate this link to a deck that explains how the URL format works: Tiny Little Office inspired me to have Claude Code (Opus 5.5 Extra) build a similar tool that works with LaTeX. Here's the sequence of prompts I used: I want a tool where you can link to it with a fragment hash #code-here and it treats that code as LaTeX and renders it really effectively. It should also support a prefix which means the latex is compressed with a custom compression dictionary optimized for latex.

Lots of details in here from " people with knowledge of the matter ". Key things I learned from the article: Q1 revenue (Jan/Feb/Mar) was "about $4.8bn" Q2 (Apr/May/Jun) was $11.5bn Q3 (Jul/Aug/Sep) was $16.8bn In Q3: $1.70 in revenue for every $1 spent on "computing power" Adjusted operating margins (excluding stock-based compensation) were 11% This point about distillation is worth quoting in full (highlight mine): The start-up has also clamped down on “distillation” — a controversial practice whereby rival AI labs train their models on the outputs of more advanced ones. When rival companies “distil” their models from Anthropic’s models, that involves heavy usage and spending.

### sparqlite: SPARQL 1.1 inside SQLite, written in Rust
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/16/sqlite-sparql-rust/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Research: sparqlite: SPARQL 1.1 inside SQLite, written in Rust This research task is really an experiment in running Claude Code for web in "Ultracode" mode, as described in Orchestrate subagents at scale with dynamic workflows in the Claude Code documentation. With Ultracode turned on, Claude Code makes aggressive use of dynamic workflows - it generates JavaScript that defines and coordinates a network of subagents to take on complex problems. My initial prompt was this - I meant "hit the rows directly", the word "delete" was an accidental typo on my phone: Write a SQLite extension in Rust that implants SPARQL and runs really fast because it can hit the delete rows directly This caused Claude Code for web to churn for over 12 hours. I checked in on its progress the next day and sent a single follow-up prompt when I noticed how many Rust crates it had built: Every folder in the crates/ directory needs a README Include a tutorial.

### Quoting Addy Osmani, Morgan Westlee Lunt, and Qing Wang
Source: Simon Willison's Weblog
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://simonwillison.net/2026/Oct/16/code-review/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

We think "did a human read the code?" is an imprecise proxy for "what could go wrong with this change, and which check—automated or human—is the right one to catch it?" Asking that question also changed when review happens. Before, review was a gate at the end of the road: write the code, open a PR, wait for your colleague. Now, review runs continuously, more like spellcheck while you write. Most changes have been reviewed and fixed by Claude at least once before a human is asked to look. This catches most routine mistakes, and saves team attention for higher-level questions and scrutiny. — Addy Osmani, Morgan Westlee Lunt, and Qing Wang , How the Claude Code team does code review Tags: anthropic , claude , ai , claude-code , llms , addy-osmani , coding-agents , generative-ai , agentic-engineering , code-review

### A report from the LPC sched_ext microconference
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-28 22:11 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1103426/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The Linux Plumbers Conference blog has a report from the sched_ext microconference held in Prague. Three main topics dominated the discussions Priority inversion and how to handle it in sched_ext : lock holder and lock waiter preemption, time slice extension, and making proxy execution work together with BPF schedulers. Paravirtualized scheduling : letting the host and guest schedulers cooperate across the VM boundary, with sched_ext as the policy engine on the host. Device and topology-aware scheduling : using hardware feedback (Intel HFI), device/accelerator locality and cache topology to drive task placement.

### Redox OS ring buffers project summary
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-28 14:05 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1103359/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The Redox OS project, which is a Unix-like microkernel-based operating system written in Rust, has published a report on the work that it has done in the last year to implement io_uring-like I/O . Last year, the Redox team received a grant to develop io_uring-like Ring Buffer support at the system task level, as a first step towards ring buffer communications between the application and the majority of system services. This phase of the work finished up in September, as the last pieces were put in place. [...] In addition to implementing the Ring Buffer machinery for our system services and drivers that can support it, an io_uring and liburing -like API for applications was completed and demonstrated. There was also substantial work done on support crates, including an async executor for drivers. And there was some very exciting work on various performance improvements, to allow much faster switching between applications and their system services. But I will let the team tell you all the technical details.

### Robinson: Raspberry Pi 5 on Fedora
Source: LWN.net
Published: 2026-10-27 18:11 UTC
URL: https://lwn.net/Articles/1103212/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Peter Robinson has written a blog post about the current state of Fedora support for Raspberry Pi 5 devices. According to Robinson, the upcoming Fedora 45 release will work—with caveats—on all variants of the Raspberry Pi Model 5B; the Compute Module 5 and Pi 500+ are still works in progress. For Fedora 44 release you need to have a temporary workaround when booting. If you update to the latest kernel/firmware straight away this isn't then needed beyond the first boot. The workaround isn't needed for Fedora 45 so if you're going to try this out I suggest you go and grab your favourite flavour of Fedora from the Fedora 45 release candidates and jump straight in there! You will get an accelerated desktop with storage/network/USB. There's not currently any support for sound and a number of other things. See Robinson's post for instructions on creating an Fedora image to use with a Raspberry Pi 5.

### Introducing the AlloyDB Omni web UI for easier cluster observability
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-28 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/databases/alloydb-omni-delivers-a-web-ui-for-cluster-observability/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Managing PostgreSQL databases across on-premises, edge, and multi-cloud environments often forces administrators to rely on command-line tools, making it hard to see cluster health and quickly diagnose issues. To solve this, we are announcing the General Availability (GA) of the AlloyDB Omni Web UI for Red Hat and containers . This production-ready, read-only console gives you direct visibility into hardware telemetry, query performance, and configuration flags for AlloyDB Omni so you can troubleshoot workloads quickly and safely without leaving your local or enterprise environment. What is AlloyDB Omni? AlloyDB Omni is a downloadable, self-managed edition of AlloyDB that brings enterprise capabilities and full PostgreSQL compatibility to your own infrastructure. Built to run anywhere — across on-premises data centers, edge environments, and multi-cloud deployments — it includes built-in columnar acceleration and integrated AI tooling.

### Near-zero downtime upgrades: Announcing Cloud SQL for MySQL blue-green deployments
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-28 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/databases/cloud-sql-blue-green-deployments/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Keeping production databases on supported versions shouldn’t require giving up your weekend. To stay secure, compliant, and with the best performance and newest AI capabilities, organizations need to run on the latest database engine versions. Yet for many teams, major version upgrades mean weeks of cross-team coordination, negotiating narrow maintenance windows, and hours of application downtime. In an era where mission-critical applications and AI agents operate as 24/7 systems of action, even a brief maintenance outage can mean disrupted customer experiences and lost revenue. To help, we are excited to announce the preview of fully managed blue-green deployments in Cloud SQL , starting with Cloud SQL for MySQL. You can now execute major version upgrades and configuration updates with minimal downtime via the Google Cloud console or automated CLI scripts. By continuously synchronizing a parallel environment, teams can safely test with production data before a seamless cutover — all while keeping the original database as a reliable safety net after the switchover.

### Behind the scenes, two months later: How we ship Google Agent Skills like products
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-28 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/developers-practitioners/behind-the-scenes-two-months-later-how-we-ship-google-agent-skills-like-products/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In my previous post, I took you behind the scenes of Google Agent Skills , covering how the project started, how we maintain quality at scale, and how we handle governance. In this post, I'll share what we've built since then: the automated quality gates that every change must pass, the AI review agent that inspects skill quality, and the lessons that we learned along the way. You can adopt similar gates and review rules in your own skill repository. Since my last post, the Google Agent Skills repository has passed 21,000 GitHub stars, up from 15,000. It now hosts over 170 skills from teams across Google, and we distribute them across multiple AI platforms. Building agent skills for a single developer or a small project is straightforward. You write a SKILL.md file, define a few instructions, test it manually, and it works. At our scale, manual workflows stop being effective. Unvetted edits can introduce subtle bugs, and it becomes difficult to catch issues before they reach users.

### How to troubleshoot GKE workloads faster with VPC Flow Logs
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-28 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/containers-kubernetes/using-vpc-flow-logs-to-troubleshoot-gke-workloads/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Having visibility into your network traffic is essential to operating Kubernetes environments effectively. When a service slows down or loses connectivity, knowing who is talking to whom lets you narrow the problem down quickly. In a standard cloud network, flow logs provide that view by recording connections between IP addresses, ports, and protocols. In Kubernetes, however, pods are temporary. Their IP addresses change constantly as pods scale, crash, or move between nodes, so a raw log entry showing 10.4.2.18 talking to 10.4.8.44 tells you little about which workload (and pod) owned that address, what namespace it was in, or what application it was trying to reach. By the time you search your cluster, the pod may already be gone! In a Google Cloud environment, VPC Flow Logs closes this gap at the network layer. Google Cloud's virtual network already sees every connection a pod makes, so each sampled flow is recorded with the Google Kubernetes Engine (GKE) metadata behind the IP address: pod, workload, namespace, and service.

### How GKE networking scales to support high densities of concurrent AI agents
Source: Google Cloud Blog
Published: 2026-10-28 16:00 UTC
URL: https://cloud.google.com/blog/products/networking/gke-networking-dataplane-for-large-agentic-ai-workloads/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

What do you need to orchestrate high-density concurrent AI agents? As we transition from static requests toward autonomous agentic workflows, the need for secure, high-density sandboxes is pushing conventional infrastructure far beyond its architectural breaking point. This transition is driving top-tier research labs to demand infrastructure at unprecedented scale — often up to one million concurrent code-execution sandboxes! At this scale, the efficiency of your Kubernetes networking infrastructure directly impacts the economic viability of an agentic initiative. In advanced workflows like reinforcement learning (RL), extreme pod churn can mean thousands of isolated sandboxes spinning up and down every minute. To make sure that valuable GPUs and TPUs are spending their time executing models rather than waiting on sandboxes to initialize, you need sub-second provisioning and extreme compute density. By providing secure, lightweight isolation, Agent Sandbox on GKE is uniquely positioned to power these highly dynamic execution environments.

The world of work is rapidly evolving, and with it, the tools we use to stay productive and connected. Today marks the launch of XREAL AURA , a first-of-kind set of wired XR glasses built on Android XR. XREAL AURA’s form factor brings new and impressive features for enterprises such as: Spatial computing on wired glasses using a newly introduced split-compute architecture. An optical-see-through display with a 70-degree field of view that layers rich visual content directly onto your physical world, allowing you to stay connected. A glasses frame weight of less than 95g, allowing for extended work sessions by employees. Most importantly, AURA brings unique productivity benefits to users. Employees will have out-of-the-box support for enterprise apps, including popular titles currently available on the Google Play Store.

### Announcing the Workload Conformance Working Group
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-27 04:30 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/27/announcing-workload-conformance-wg/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We are excited to announce that the Kubernetes community now has a Workload Conformance Working Group . The working group is writing a shared standard for how workloads behave at runtime on Kubernetes. To get it right, we need input from the people who build those workloads and from the people who run the clusters they're deployed on. Why workloads need a shared standard Certified Kubernetes tests the cluster, and Kubernetes AI Conformance tests whether a cluster can run AI and ML workloads. Nothing comparable exists for the workloads themselves, so cluster operators still check each application by hand before routine maintenance or a Kubernetes upgrade. The problems they find are familiar. A routine node drain is often how you discover that an application can't tolerate disruption. An upgrade can break an application that still depends on a removed API version . Others show up in day-to-day operation: workloads that ask for privileges they don't need, or reserve resources they never use. Kubernetes already documents much of this, including security and handling disruptions .

### Making Failure Diagnosis Reliable with Pensive AI
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-26 17:04 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/making-failure-diagnosis-reliable-with-pensive-ai-6b0811a1c015?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

By Praneeth Yenugutala , Victor Dusa , Anastasia Samoilova , Megan Ren , Justin Wei , Marcelo Mayworm At Netflix, data and machine learning practitioners run millions of workloads each day across orchestration systems and compute engines such as Titus, Spark, Trino, and Druid. On peak days, roughly 100,000 workloads fail. When workloads fail, the failure itself is only the beginning. Someone still needs to find the useful signal in several logs, decide whether the cause sits in their code or the platform, and determine whether to retry, reconfigure, or escalate. Even a small share of difficult failures can consume engineering time, waste compute, and interrupt critical data pipelines. Our first answer to that problem was Pensive , an auto-diagnosis and remediation system in Netflix’s data platform. Pensive collected logs from failed workloads, used regular expressions and hand-authored Java rules to extract and classify errors, then passed retry or remediation guidance to products such as Maestro . It made ~ 50% of failures self-service and, in some cases, automatic.

Source: Netflix TechBlog

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

By Roberto Perez Alcolea , Java Platform Team Quasar connects a proposed API change with observed references in consumer repositories, giving the producer evidence for a release decision At Netflix, shared Java libraries support applications developed across a large, independently evolving repository ecosystem. A library change begins in one repository, but its impact can reach many others. Producers can see the API diff in front of them. What they often cannot see is who still depends on the symbols that changed. The need for that visibility grows as coding agents take on more of the software development lifecycle. An agent can accelerate a local edit, but without cross-repository context it cannot judge downstream risk or help plan consumer migrations. The evidence a producer uses to assess a change can also guide the agent working on it. Consider a common case: removing a deprecated API. Deprecated entry points expand the surface producers must test, document, and support, and they can constrain how a library evolves.

### Single-tenant Titus: VM-level isolation on a multi-tenant container platform
Source: Netflix TechBlog
Published: 2026-10-22 22:19 UTC
URL: https://netflixtechblog.com/single-tenant-titus-vm-level-isolation-on-a-multi-tenant-container-platform-6d8b1909d163?source=rss----2615bd06b42e---4
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Samartha Chandrashekar & Nick Parker You click play on a Friday night, and in the second that follows, dozens of services answer in milliseconds. Most of them run on Titus, our internal container platform. Titus is, in essence, a Kubernetes-based container hosting platform: we keep both the control plane and the data plane (the container hosts that actually run containers) patched and up to date, so application teams get a consistent and reliable container experience. They hand us a container, and we run it, with no host, cluster, or kernel to own. Titus launches millions of containers every week across tens of thousands of EC2 instances in multiple AWS regions. It is multi-tenant by default: many containers from many teams share each host. That density is what makes container compute efficient, and it is why Titus has been the default home for so much of Netflix’s compute for years. For most workloads, sharing a host works well. For a few, it does not, and those few include some of the most demanding real-time systems we run.

### Why Is My Kubernetes Job Still Pending? Tracing Kueue Admission with Headlamp
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-14 18:35 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/14/tracing-kueue-admission-with-headlamp/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

You submit a Kubernetes Job and wait for its Pods to appear. A minute passes. Then another. The Job is still suspended, there are no application logs, and there may not even be a Pod to inspect. In a cluster managed by Kueue , this can be completely normal. Kueue may still be deciding whether the Job can use the requested resources. The useful question is not only “Why is the Pod pending?” It is first “Has Kueue admitted the Workload?” The Kueue plugin for Headlamp brings that investigation into the Headlamp UI. Instead of reconstructing the admission path from several commands and large YAML objects, you can begin with the Workload, read its condition message, and follow links through the LocalQueue, ClusterQueue, ResourceFlavor, and Cohort. This article follows that Headlamp-first path. The command line appears only in a short optional section for reproducing the example. Before you begin You need a Kubernetes cluster with Kueue installed , a Headlamp installation , and the 0.1.0-alpha Kueue plugin .

### The Shift to cgroup v2 in Kubernetes: What You Need to Know
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-06 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/06/kubernetes-cgroups-v2-shift/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In Linux, cgroups (control groups) are a kernel feature used for managing system resources. Kubernetes uses cgroups to allocate resources like CPU and memory to containers, ensuring that applications run smoothly without interfering with each other. With the release of Kubernetes v1.31, support for v1 cgroup management moved into maintenance mode . Support for v2 cgroup management has been stable since Kubernetes v1.25. Compared with cgroup v1, cgroup v2 provides a single unified hierarchy, a more consistent interface, and a stronger foundation for resource isolation and modern resource-management features. Deprecation of cgroup v1 Kubernetes has deprecated cgroup v1 . Starting with Kubernetes v1.35, failCgroupV1 defaults to true , so the kubelet does not start on a cgroup v1 node by default. Administrators can temporarily set failCgroupV1: false in the kubelet configuration file , but removal will follow the Kubernetes deprecation policy . Further removal work is tracked in KEP-5573: Remove cgroup v1 support . If you are still on a release older than v1.

### Scaling Kubernetes Workloads with Node Swap
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-10-05 18:00 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/10/05/scaling-kubernetes-workloads-with-node-swap/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Memory is often the first hard limit a Kubernetes cluster hits. Nodes run out of RAM long before they run out of CPU, and the new wave of agentic AI workloads makes this worse. These workloads demand large memory footprints to start up and run untrusted code, then sit idle waiting for the next prompt. That idle but resident memory is expensive, and it caps how many pods a node can hold. This is where swap helps. Kubernetes support for running nodes with swap enabled reached General Availability in v1.34, and by backing that swap with fast NVMe solid state drives (SSDs), a node can page out dormant memory and pack in far more pods. This post explains how we benchmarked that approach across three workloads, including CI/CD kernel builds, sandboxed headless browsers, and isolated Python runtimes; we found density gains of up to 3×, often with little or no latency cost.

Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

As Kubernetes adoption has grown, the conversation has shifted beyond running containers to managing increasingly complex application lifecycles. Modern platforms support stateless web services, stateful databases, batch processing, AI workloads, and platform services. At the same time, they must remain reliable during upgrades, scaling events, and infrastructure failures. Every Kubernetes user relies on SIG Apps, whether they realize it or not. Deployments, StatefulSets, DaemonSets, Jobs, and CronJobs form the foundation of how applications are deployed, updated, scaled, and operated across the Kubernetes ecosystem. SIG Apps is focused on improving workload resilience, refining application lifecycle management, and addressing the operational challenges that emerge when applications encounter node failures, rollout disruptions, and increasingly complex infrastructure environments.

### Kubernetes v1.37: Tracking When a PersistentVolumeClaim Was Last Used (Beta)
Source: Kubernetes Blog
Published: 2026-09-21 18:30 UTC
URL: https://kubernetes.io/blog/2026/09/21/kubernetes-v1-37-pvc-last-used-time/
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Kubernetes v1.37 promotes the PersistentVolumeClaimUnusedSinceTime feature gate to Beta (enabled by default). With this feature, the PersistentVolumeClaim (PVC) protection controller adds an Unused condition to each PVC, telling you whether any running pod currently references it — no custom tooling or cross-referencing required. For the API definition of PVC conditions, see the PersistentVolumeClaim API reference . Read on to learn how the Unused condition works and how to use it. Why track PVC usage? In large-scale Kubernetes clusters, it is common for users to create PVCs and then delete the associated pods without cleaning up the storage, because Kubernetes does not automatically delete PVCs when their pods are removed (to protect against accidental data loss). Over time, these orphaned PVCs may accumulate, silently consuming storage capacity and driving up cloud costs. Before Kubernetes v1.37, it was easy to identify an unused PersistentVolume, but much harder to determine whether a PVC was still being used.

### How Beta Technologies Is Clearing the Runway for Electric Flight
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-28 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/beta-technologies-electric-aircraft
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In the cockpit of Beta Technologies’ Alia CX300, pilot Chris Caputo steers onto Runway 33 at Patrick Leahy Burlington International Airport in Vermont. Cleared for takeoff, he releases the brakes and nudges the throttle forward. At the back of the aircraft, the five-blade pusher propeller snaps awake, and the Alia bolts forward. There’s no shriek of a turbofan spooling up, no shudder of a piston engine, and no wait for thrust. Just the low hum of an electric motor and a shove of instant acceleration. Within a few seconds, we’re at 85 knots (almost 160 kilometers per hour), and Caputo rotates the nose upward. The runway drops abruptly away as we climb. Gaining speed and altitude, we head northwest over Lake Champlain. Through the wraparound canopy, I watch the hunched shoulders of Vermont’s Green Mountains shrink under the right wing, with New York’s taller Adirondacks to the left. Caputo, a former Air Force fighter pilot, flicks the control stick hard left, then right.

### FAA Gets Serious About Aviation Airwaves
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-28 08:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/faa-aviation-airwaves
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The skies are becoming dangerously crowded—not with planes but with interference and jamming disrupting many of the 30,000 radio frequencies that civil aviation relies upon. In previous decades, designers of aviation weather, navigation, and voice communication systems prioritized convenience over security, leaving today’s aircraft vulnerable to both unwitting interference and malicious jamming and spoofing, especially near conflict zones . Driven by growing threats and a critical U.S. Government Accountability Office report , the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) is now seeking ambitious monitoring and detection systems to protect its spectrum nationwide. At an industry engagement day in mid-October, the FAA’s vice president of technical operations, Dan Murphy , laid out the problem: “Right now, somewhere in the National Airspace System, a signal that aviation depends upon is being jammed, being spoofed, or just being degraded by something else happening in the spectrum.

### Will Fiber-Optic Cables Finally Get Made in Orbit?
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-26 12:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/zblan-fiber-optic-cables-iss
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In a lab in Luxembourg in 2024, engineers at Flawless Photonics unpacked a spool of glass fiber that had just returned from the International Space Station (ISS). As they threaded it onto their rewinding machine, the machine jammed—as happens sometimes—and they braced for the usual outcome: The fiber would snap. This time, though, nothing broke. It was an early hint that their experiment might have been successful. The Flawless Photonics team had set out to draw fibers of a heavy-metal fluoride glass called ZBLAN—named for its other components of zirconium, barium, lanthanum, aluminum, and sodium (chemical symbol Na)—in microgravity, aiming to achieve superior optical and physical properties. “We realized, wow, that fiber is much stronger than the stuff we had produced on Earth,” says Hubert Moser , the company’s cofounder and CTO. ZBLAN Fiber’s Microgravity Advantages Photonics engineers have been intrigued by ZBLAN for decades because it can, in theory, transmit light with 1 percent of the loss of silica.

### The Robot I Didn’t Expect to Build
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-19 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/agility-robotics-digit-5-redesign
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Agility Robotics , the company I cofounded out of my robotics lab at Oregon State University in 2015, has just launched Digit 5 . It’s designed to be the first humanoid robot that can safely and economically do useful work at scale, allowing us to bring tens of thousands of humanoid workers into the real world. Engineered to lift 23-kilogram bins onto high shelves and with a battery that can run for 90 minutes on a 9-minute charge, Digit 5’s goal is to handle heavy warehouse tasks shift after shift, filling the need for labor in jobs that are too physically stressful or too dull for humans to want to do. Digit 5 is what Agility has been working toward for over a decade. But the robot is heavy and bulky, lacking the graceful natural motion of its predecessors, and it’s farther than I thought possible from the robot I dreamed of building when I started this company. To deliver a useful commercial product, Agility has taken a path that is very different from what I first imagined.

### Hera Mission Prepares for Follow-Up Asteroid Imaging
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-18 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/esa-hera-dimorphos-rendezvous
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In one month, the European Space Agency’s Hera mission will make history. Moving at 12 kilometers per second relative to Earth, the mission is about to rendezvous with the binary Dimorphos-Didymos asteroid pair. NASA’s Double Asteroid Redirection Test (DART) mission slammed into Dimorphos in 2022, and now Hera will provide a follow-up view of the mission’s resulting handiwork. The European Space Agency (ESA) recently released a thought-provoking look at the perspectives of Hera and what an astronaut might see from the surface of the asteroids as the mission speeds by. But what would it be like to hitch a ride aboard Hera? Launched on a SpaceX Falcon 9 rocket from Cape Canaveral, Fla., on 7 October 2024, Hera is ESA’s first Space Safety Programme-class mission. The spacecraft took just over two years to reach its destination. Even now, the pair of asteroids is little more than a few pixels across in Hera’s view. The mission imaged Didymos in July 2026.

### Q&A: Mars Perseverance Rover Sets a Distance Record
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-13 15:02 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/mars-rover-distance-record
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

On 11 October , the Perseverance rover completed a drive that brought its total distance traveled on Mars to 28.10 miles (45.22 kilometers). This is a new record for Mars rovers, enabled in large part by Perseverance ’s ability to drive with much more autonomy than its predecessors. Over the past five years, Perseverance ’s autonomy software has been steadily upgraded, and to learn more about how the record was broken and what the future holds for the rover, we spoke with Mark Maimone , the Perseverance Robotic Operations Deputy Team Chief at NASA’s Jet Propulsion Laboratory, who designed and developed the self-driving software for Mars rovers Spirit, Opportunity, Curiosity, and Perseverance. Before we talk about Perseverance, let’s pour one out for the previous record holder, Opportunity , which landed on Mars in January 2004 for a three-month mission and ended its epic journey 15 years later in February 2019.

### NASA Wants More Than 70 Moon Landings in the Next Decade—But How?
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-12 15:27 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/nasa-moon-base-landings
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

At Firefly Aerospace in Texas, engineers have been doing what NASA’s chief, Jared Isaacman, likes to call “the near-impossible.” The Firefly team has been assembling Blue Ghost Mission 2 , a robotic spacecraft intended to land on the far side of the moon in 2027. No U.S. spacecraft has ever gone there. Not very many have landed on the near side, either, for that matter. Blue Ghost 2 has thousands of potential failure points, and there is almost no room for error. Two ships sent by a competitor, Houston-based Intuitive Machines , reached the moon’s near side in 2024 and 2025 but fell over on landing and returned little useful data. In 2024, another mission, Peregrine Mission One , by Pittsburgh-based Astrobotic Technology , missed the moon entirely because a single helium valve wouldn’t seat properly. In summary: Moon landings are supremely hard. So far, since Project Apollo ended in 1972, U.S. spacecraft have landed intact exactly…once.

### Lunar Mission Will Explore Old Lava Tubes as Potential Habitats
Source: IEEE Spectrum Aerospace
Published: 2026-10-11 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/nasa-moon-mission-gimli-artemis
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The chief aim of NASA’s Artemis Program is to create a “sustained program of lunar exploration and development.” Beyond returning astronauts to the moon for the first time since the Apollo era , NASA and other space agencies hope to build the infrastructure that will enable regular, long-duration missions to the lunar surface. In addition to targeting the South Pole-Aitken Basin , NASA is eyeing lunar caves as a potential site for habitats and other lunar facilities. Meanwhile, space agencies need to send large amounts of cargo and robotic explorers to the surface. This includes the Geophysical Instruments for Marius Lunar pit Investigation (GIMLI), a Planetary Science Institute –led proposal that will investigate a “skylight,” or a hole in an old lunar lava tube, to see if a large cave system extends beyond it . Such a location could provide a safe place for astronauts to operate on the surface in the future. The mission team will be led by Than Putzig , an associate director and senior scientist at PSI.

### This Disembodied Hand Is All the Robot You Need
Source: IEEE Spectrum Robotics
Published: 2026-10-08 12:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/walking-robotic-hand
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A hand attached to the end of an arm is a given for most humans, but what if robots didn’t have to play by the same rules as we do? Researchers from ETH Zurich have turned an off-the-shelf robotic hand into a completely self-contained robot that walks on its fingertips while using those same fingers to manipulate its environment. Besides making the perfect Halloween accessory for anyone planning to dress up as a member of the Addams Family—who were famously accompanied by a disembodied walking hand called Thing —this robotic hand could make it possible to operate controls and handle objects in places a traditional robot arm can’t reach, the researchers say. Soft Robotics Lab, ETH Zurich/YouTube Amirhossein Kazemipour , a Ph.D. candidate in the Soft Robotics Lab at ETH Zurich who led the project, believes this hand could prove useful in contexts like fiddly maintenance tasks in industrial settings, or in search and rescue, where a robot could detach its hand to explore confined spaces too tight for the rest of its body.

BeagleBoard.org has introduced the BeagleConnect QuadESC, an open-source 4-channel BLDC motor controller/carrier board built around the BeagleConnect Torque SoM. BeagleConnect Torque SoM The Torque SoM can also be integrated with custom carrier boards for robotics, motor control, and CAN-connected applications. It provides the processing, sensing, and communication interfaces, while external hardware supplies the motor power .

### Systems Thinking Is Vital in the Age of AI
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-28 18:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/systems-thinking-vital-in-ai
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Modern engineering projects increasingly combine human judgment with machine capability. An engineer can define a problem, then ask an artificial intelligence tool to search relevant research and technical standards, generate design options, test them in simulation, and compare the results. The engineer can then bring the results to specialists who understand the parts AI cannot judge on its own, such as whether a design can be built safely, whether it makes sense in a particular setting, or how it might affect its users. The final decision about whether and how to move forward remains a human responsibility. AI can help engineers do early project steps that once required several people with different technical skills. The engineer’s role is therefore expanding from performing each technical step to deciding how people, machines, and specialists’ knowledge should work together on the project. The problem, not the title Engineering has always been divided into disciplines, and for good reasons.

### How Beta Technologies Is Clearing the Runway for Electric Flight
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-28 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/beta-technologies-electric-aircraft
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In the cockpit of Beta Technologies’ Alia CX300, pilot Chris Caputo steers onto Runway 33 at Patrick Leahy Burlington International Airport in Vermont. Cleared for takeoff, he releases the brakes and nudges the throttle forward. At the back of the aircraft, the five-blade pusher propeller snaps awake, and the Alia bolts forward. There’s no shriek of a turbofan spooling up, no shudder of a piston engine, and no wait for thrust. Just the low hum of an electric motor and a shove of instant acceleration. Within a few seconds, we’re at 85 knots (almost 160 kilometers per hour), and Caputo rotates the nose upward. The runway drops abruptly away as we climb. Gaining speed and altitude, we head northwest over Lake Champlain. Through the wraparound canopy, I watch the hunched shoulders of Vermont’s Green Mountains shrink under the right wing, with New York’s taller Adirondacks to the left. Caputo, a former Air Force fighter pilot, flicks the control stick hard left, then right.

### Your Robot’s Safety Functions Already Work. What If the Input Lies?
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-27 16:14 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/ai-robot-safety-cybersecurity-vicone
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

This article is brought to you by VicOne . A mobile robot slows for someone in a hallway. A collaborative arm eases its movement as a worker approaches. A humanoid pauses to let a person pass. Each response depends on information about the robot or its surroundings: a distance reading, a position estimate, or a stop signal. But what happens when one of those inputs is wrong, and the robot still responds exactly as designed? The mobile robot reads the hallway as clear while someone is still there. The arm estimates that the worker is farther away. The humanoid does not receive a stop signal as a person crosses its path. In each case, a fault could cause that mismatch. So could deliberate manipulation . At VicOne LAB R7 , we examine how manipulated inputs can change robot behavior — and what that means for the systems meant to keep people safe. How an untrusted input can change a robot’s behavior A robot does more than measure its surroundings. It may interpret what it sees and hears to decide what to do next. Information placed in its environment can therefore influence its actions.

### I Used AI to Fake a Scientific Paper. Here’s What Happened
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-27 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/ai-generated-scientific-papers
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Science has a spam problem. And the spam filter is getting clogged up. According to all reports, academic publishing is drowning in what has come to be called AI slop: low-quality, mass-produced content generated with AI. By 2025, an estimated 57 percent of all published academic articles exhibited detectable evidence of large language model (LLM) influence, a massive jump from 12 percent in 2023. What’s more, over 12 percent of Nature Communications reviews were found to be completely AI-generated in 2025. Given that for every 40 papers showing statistical evidence of AI usage in 2025, only one formally disclosed it , these figures likely grossly underestimate LLM involvement today. Of course, evidence of AI use doesn’t necessarily mean a paper is slop. But some estimates suggest that hundreds of thousands of fake, often AI-generated, publications are now produced annually. “It’s not just the [scientific] record under threat, it’s science as a whole,” warns Amanda Sulicz , IEEE’s research-integrity manager.

Source: CNX Software

MYIR MYC-JR3576 is a SMARC 2.2 system-on-module (SoM) powered by a Rockchip RK3576/RK3576J octa-core Cortex-A72/A53 SoC with a 6 TOPS NPU designed for Edge AI applications. It features up to 8GB LPDDR5, up to 64GB eMMC flash, an optional WiFI 6E and Bluetooth 5.4 module, and exposes all I/Os through a 314-pin MXM connector as defined in the SMARC 2.2 standard. The company says it’s the socketed counterpart to its LGA-packaged MYC-LR3576 (43 x 45 mm) module introduced in 2024, so the same processor is now available in both solder-down and standard form factors from MYiR. ### IEEE Xplore Digital Library Adds AI Assistant
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-26 18:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/ieee-xplore-digital-library-ai
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

IEEE recently released the IEEE Xplore AI Research Suite , built on the full text of millions of peer-reviewed publications in the IEEE Xplore Digital Library . The library offers full-text access to IEEE standards , journals, magazines, conference proceedings, and more. Researchers who use the AI suite to search the digital library receive answers grounded in verified scientific results, ensuring a high level of accuracy and trust. The suite is available as an optional subscription upgrade to all IEEE Xplore Digital Library institutional subscribers. Powered by a proprietary AI system optimized for STEM literature, the suite introduces powerful, intelligent features within the digital library. The suite enables users to seamlessly search and discover articles to accelerate their comprehension of complex technical papers. The system surfaces deep insights, replacing the typical keyword queries with natural, conversational research workflows.

### Build a Hidden Lock With a Magnetic Imager
Source: IEEE Spectrum
Published: 2026-10-26 13:00 UTC
URL: https://spectrum.ieee.org/magnetic
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Like many a nerd, I have a certain fascination with locks. This originates in reading the memoir Surely You’re Joking, Mr. Feynman as a teenager, in which Richard Feynman recounts picking locks and cracking combinations while at his day job working on the Manhattan Project at Los Alamos. I currently have a couple of locksport pick sets and training locks that I purchased at hacker conferences over the years (although I’m still terrible at it), and I delight in the YouTube exploits of folks like the LockPickingLawyer . All of which left me wondering if I could try building an interesting lock. One dodge that lockmakers have long used to challenge would-be plunderers is to conceal the existence of the lock itself. A modern variant of this is the magnetic lock, where a magnet is held or slid against an innocuous portion of a door or box, releasing a catch. The problem with this is that, once you guess the location of the lock, any strong magnet will open it.

### Training AIs to Solve CAPTCHAs
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/training-ais-to-solve-captchas.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

Interesting research : Abstract : Vision-language models (VLMs) can solve visual CAPTCHAs without task-specific training, but the agents built on them approach every challenge from scratch. For such an agent, the hundredth instance of a familiar puzzle costs as much time and compute as the first. Specialized detectors invert the trade-off, answering in milliseconds but only for categories they were trained on. Neither improves with exposure. We study what changes when a solver improves with use. Our system pairs a fine-tuned YOLOv8 detector with an open-weight VLM behind a confidence-based router, and runs entirely from screenshots and operating-system input events, with no browser automation or DOM access. It reaches 85.4% overall and 84.2% macro accuracy across 16 classes, exceeding either component alone. Every answer VLM produces also serves as a training label, so the detector absorbs categories it was never trained for, typically after one or two encounters and without human annotation. The same loop also repairs it.

URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/new-serious-php-vulnerabilities-released-and-patched.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

PHP maintainers have released fixes for two critical vulnerabilities: PHP 8.5.12, 8.4.27, 8.3.36 and 8.2.35 fix two critical vulnerabilities that can allow unauthenticated remote code execution. All PHP users are strongly encouraged to update immediately. Who is at risk: all PHP installations serving web requests, including PHP-FPM, Apache with mod_php, php-cgi, FrankenPHP and LiteSpeed. We consider the risk highest for mod_php, with a demonstrated exploit where an unauthenticated request can trigger the attack before any application code runs...

### Compromised TLS Certificates
Source: Schneier on Security
Published: 2026-10-29 03:53 UTC
URL: https://www.schneier.com/blog/archives/2026/10/compromised-tls-certificates.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R5 · P5 · baseline

The Indian CA eMudhra Limited has eight TLS certificates with laughably weak private keys : The compromised eMudhra keys were defective at a level that’s challenging the imagination of many of the engineers studying the incident. A normal size for a NIST P-384 elliptic curve cryptography private key contains roughly 384 bits of entropy, a size allowing almost 2 384 , or a number with 116 digits, different possibilities. The private key sizes of the compromised keys ranged from 3 to 8 bits. Astonishingly, the private keys were 4, 56, 66, 115, 155, 156, 230, 237...

### Victory! You Can Now Exclude Apps from Siri AI
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-28 20:04 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/victory-you-can-now-exclude-apps-siri-ai
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

In the most recent update to Apple’s devices, iOS 27.2, the company has introduced a new setting to “Exclude Apps” from Siri AI, fulfilling one of our asks from our Encrypt It Already campaign earlier this year . One of our main concerns with new AI features on devices is how those features interact with end-to-end encrypted content, particularly with secure messaging apps. Many AI features require cloud processing, and despite privacy protections engineered into these systems , the content of secure messaging apps may leave a user’s device without their knowledge. That changes the privacy concerns around using those tools. In a perfect world, users would be able to put a barrier up to ensure data from certain apps doesn’t leave the device. Here was our initial ask: You should be able to tell the operating system’s AI to not access an app, even if that comes at the “cost” of missing out on some features. The setting should be straightforward and easy to understand in ways the Gemini and Apple Intelligence controls currently are not.

### Court Should Curb Abuse of Grand Jury Subpoenas Targeting Social Media Users
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-28 19:48 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/court-should-curb-abuse-grand-jury-subpoenas-targeting-social-media-users
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A federal appeals court should curb the government’s abuse of grand jury subpoenas to unmask social media users who criticize lawless immigration enforcement. That is the message from the ACLU, EFF, and other civil liberties and trade groups who filed a friend-of-the-court brief in a sealed case in the U.S. Court of Appeals for the District of Columbia. The case involves the government’s investigation of an anonymous Reddit user who commented on a post about an immigration officer in Minnesota. The Department of Homeland Security (DHS) first issued an administrative subpoena to Reddit to unmask the user. When the user challenged the subpoena in court on free speech and statutory grounds ( as many others have ), DHS withdrew it. But then the government obtained a separate grand jury subpoena—a subpoena issued by a prosecutor to gather evidence to present to a grand jury before any criminal charges are filed—for much of the same information. The user then challenged the grand jury subpoena too, and the case is currently being appealed. The right to speak anonymously is essential.

EFF and other groups are fighting back. In our latest EFFector newsletter , we're talking about a recent victory in our lawsuit against the government for sharing federal workers' confidential personnel files with DOGE . JOIN OUR NEWSLETTER For over 35 years, EFFector has been your guide to understanding the intersection of technology, civil liberties, and the law. This issue covers our free tracker blocker coming to Safari, a call to action for net neutrality in California , and a big win in our lawsuit against the DOGE data grab. Prefer to listen in? ### Social Media When Elections Are Close: Doing the Impossible Better
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-27 21:20 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/social-media-when-elections-are-close-doing-impossible-better
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Everyone knows, or should, that perfect (balanced, targeted, transparent, swift and swiftly appealable) content moderation at scale is impossible. That doesn’t mean powerful social platforms don’t have to try to get at least reasonably close, especially when access to good information can have a profound effect on political participation and speech. That’s why so many people, including the Meta Oversight Board , criticized Meta for suspending a major political Instagram account for hours during a huge early voting push on October 17; and for allegedly boosting one candidate in Brazil’s recent presidential election. Meta denies deliberate interference in either instance, blaming “mistakes” in its automated content moderation systems (including AI tools that can’t tell the difference between enthusiastic follower engagement and a bot attack). Mistakes or not, with early voting in the U.S.

### New Records Obtained by EFF Expose More Problems with Medicare’s AI Experiment
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-27 19:05 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/new-records-obtained-eff-expose-more-problems-medicares-ai-experiment
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

New records obtained by EFF expose additional issues with Medicare’s AI-driven prior authorization program and show why we need stronger legal limits around government use of automated decision-making . EFF sued the government back in March for information about the Wasteful and Inappropriate Service Reduction (WISeR) model , a new Medicare program that uses AI to evaluate prior authorization requests for certain medical services. We previously released records showing that WISeR has resulted in delays and denials of care, operational chaos, and reports of patient harm. Today, we’re publishing over 800 new pages of records, including: Requests for information (RFIs) or requests for corrective action plans (CAPs) sent from the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) to four of the six WISeR vendors, along with the vendors’ responses. Applications of 66 companies to the WISeR model, including those of the 6 selected companies. Task orders and contracts for the evaluation, implementation, and monitoring of the WISeR model.

### French Court’s Site-Blocking Orders Drive Quad9 Out Of France
Source: Electronic Frontier Foundation
Published: 2026-10-27 16:19 UTC
URL: https://www.eff.org/deeplinks/2026/10/french-courts-site-blocking-orders-drive-quad9-out-france
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A nonprofit service that protects internet users’ privacy is leaving France. Quad9, a free DNS resolver, has said it will reluctantly stop serving users in France on November 24. This follows a French court order that Quad9 must block access to websites that are accused of infringing the copyrights of a sports broadcaster. Forcing a responsible privacy service out of an important market is a predictable result of trying to force infrastructure providers to be copyright police. When you visit a website, a DNS resolver reads the domain name you type in—like eff.org — and translates it into a numerical IP address that’s used to direct data across the internet. A DNS resolver can see the names of websites you connect to, so its privacy policies are important. Quad9 is an NGO that’s incorporated in Switzerland, in part to protect its users from data demands. The company also helps protect users from malware and phishing by blocking domains that are known to be malicious.

### Out Now: Low-tech Magazine Volume IV
Source: Low-Tech Magazine
Published: 2026-09-24 00:00 UTC
URL: https://solar.lowtechmagazine.com/2026/09/out-now-low-tech-magazine-volume-iv/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Image: Low-tech Magazine Volume IV (2022-2026). Photo: Juliette Demaret. Low-tech Magazine presents the fourth volume in the chronological book series. It contains twenty articles that were published online between 2022 and 2026. Three earlier volumes collect articles written and published online between 2007 and 2021, which are also bundled in the “ Compressed Edition ”. This new volume is a bit different from the earlier books: eight of the articles are DIY guides or manuals, and we have grouped them together in the second part of the book. This volume covers the period that Marie Verdeil joined Low-tech Magazine, which partly shifted the focus from writing articles to building prototypes inspired by historical research. These prototypes also led us to conduct workshops in universities and participate in museum exhibitions. As is the case with all of our books, the articles have been edited and improved compared to the original web versions. Some articles gained extra or better images.

### Rediscovering the Handcart
Source: Low-Tech Magazine
Published: 2026-04-22 00:00 UTC
URL: https://solar.lowtechmagazine.com/2026/04/rediscovering-the-handcart/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Image: The handcart, equipped with a sail. Photo by Kris De Decker. The human-powered handcart is the oldest of vehicles, and it will likely be the last one around in the future. Of all vehicles, it’s the cheapest and least complex to build and use. It offers a large advantage over carrying a load on your back or dragging it over the ground - the even older concept of the sled. On the other hand, the handcart is cheaper and easier to use than the animal-powered cart. Oxen and donkeys eat more than humans, and they have their own will, which can work against the driver. Like any other wheeled vehicle, the handcart requires roads to drive on. This infrastructure has not always been available anywhere or at any time in history. For example, in medieval Europe, porters and pack animals were more common than handcarts because of poor roads. 1 In the West, the handcart only reached its heyday during the first decades of the Industrial Revolution, when it connected fast-growing cities to train stations and harbors.

### Low-tech Magazine: The Uncompressed Book Series
Source: Low-Tech Magazine
Published: 2026-03-30 00:00 UTC
URL: https://solar.lowtechmagazine.com/2026/03/low-tech-magazine-the-uncompressed-book-series/
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Image: The redesigned and revised chronological book edition. Photo: Marie Verdeil. Between 2019 and 2021, Low-tech Magazine published three books containing selections of articles from the website, spanning 14 years (2007-2021). In 2024, we launched the “compressed edition” , which squeezes the article catalog of the three-volume book series into just one book of 620 pages. We did this by switching to a smaller font size, downsizing most images, and opting for a two-column layout. I rewrote some articles, especially older ones, resulting not only in fewer pages but also in better articles. This revised edition of the original, “uncompressed” series is based on the edits made for the compressed edition. However, we increased the font and image sizes and returned to a one-column layout for improved reading comfort. The book design is a collaborative effort by Laia Comellas, Marie Verdeil, and Johanna Gratzer. Marie Verdeil made the covers, Vaiva Vinskaitė did the typesetting. Image: The redesigned and revised chronological book edition. Photo: Marie Verdeil.

### Hackers, Forklifts, and Other Things Manufacturers Blame on Repair
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2026-05-11 18:06 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2026/5/11/manufacturers-have-a-new-argument-against-repair-everyone-else-is-too-dangerous
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

When lawmakers consider Right to Repair, manufacturers and their trade groups usually show up with the same script. They support repair, of course. They support consumer choice, naturally. They support small businesses, safety, sustainability, affordability, resilience, and probably puppies. They just oppose the actual law that would make repair possible. That script is on full display in Alaska, where HB 162 would give Alaskans more practical access to the parts, tools, and information needed to fix the products they own. And the opposition testimony has been revealing. Not because the arguments are new, but because they are so familiar, so exaggerated, and so openly hostile to the basic idea that anyone outside a manufacturer-controlled network can be trusted to repair anything. The message from industry is clear: repair is fine, as long as manufacturers get to decide who repairs, what gets repaired, when repair happens, and how much control they keep after the sale. That is not repair freedom. That is a service monopoly with better lighting.

### Cisco Is Hiding Its Repair Monopoly Behind “Security”
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2026-05-04 16:20 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2026/5/4/cisco-is-hiding-its-repair-monopoly-behind-security
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

It took years of pressure, but consumers and advocates forced Apple to let people fix their own phones. That fight is not over. It just moved. Now the targets include everything from McFlurry machines to John Deere tractors to enterprise hardware from Cisco Systems. And right now, Cisco is leading the pack in the race to be the least repair-friendly major OEM. Over the past two weeks in Colorado, Cisco, IBM, and Hewlett-Packard Enterprise helped unleash more than 20 lobbying firms to kill the right to repair for businesses and government. Their argument was simple. If you do not use OEM parts, you are putting national security at risk. This is not just wrong. It is fiction. Since the first right to repair bill in 2014, not a single real-world example has been produced to support this claim. Not one. No breach. No incident. No evidence. Just hypotheticals dressed up as policy. Instead, we get scenarios that sound like action movies, not reality. They also tried to argue that repair should be restricted for equipment used in nuclear facilities and other sensitive environments.

### A Manufacturer’s Guide to Right to Repair Compliance
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-10-18 13:20 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/10/18/a-manufacturers-guide-to-right-to-repair-compliance
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

Overview: The New Reality of Repair With Right to Repair laws now active in states representing over a third of the U.S. market, non-compliance is no longer a viable option. For manufacturers, this shift presents a critical choice: treat repair as a burdensome legal obligation or embrace it as a strategic opportunity to build customer loyalty, open new revenue streams, and enhance brand reputation. This guide provides a clear roadmap for navigating the new legal landscape. The Legal Foundation of Right to Repair Resistance to Right to Repair statutes has often been based on flawed legal arguments that have consistently proven ineffective. The legal framework supporting repair is built on long-standing principles: Basic Ownership Law (the First Sale Doctrine) : Many manufacturers have attempted to maintain control over products post-sale through End-User License Agreements (EULAs). However, legal precedent is clear: the transfer of ownership is complete at the point of sale . The new owner assumes responsibility for the product’s safety, security, and privacy.

### The Repair Association Backs Military Right to Repair (S.2209)
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-07-21 17:57 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/7/21/the-repair-association-backs-military-right-to-repair-s2209
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

The fight for the Right to Repair has reached a critical milestone in Washington. The Senate Armed Services Committee is reviewing S.2209 , a groundbreaking bill that would guarantee our military’s right to repair the equipment they purchase and rely on. Today, we submitted a letter to Chairman Roger Wicker , Ranking Member Jack Reed , Senator Tim Sheehy , Senator Elizabeth Warren , and all members of the committee, voicing our full support for S.2209. This legislation would eliminate unnecessary repair monopolies, ensuring our armed forces can fix critical equipment on their own terms. Why S.2209 Matters Our military is the largest buyer of tech-enabled products in the world—yet too often, it’s locked out of essential repairs because manufacturers withhold access to parts, tools, or diagnostic information. These restrictions slow down readiness, inflate costs, and undermine the very concept of ownership. Repair isn’t about copying designs or exposing trade secrets. It’s a fundamental right tied to ownership, and it’s essential for operational readiness.

### Celebrating 50 States on the Right to Repair
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-02-24 10:00 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/2/24/ptkkw1yziw8xv7u9iwhoohl42ojyvu
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

As of last week, all 50 states are now actively participating in the fight for our collective right to repair. Notably, Wisconsin and New Mexico introduced repair legislation for the first time, filling the final gaps on our nationwide map. Meanwhile, states like New York , Oregon , and Minnesota continue to enhance their existing laws, marking steady progress across the country. Our signature color scheme of black, red, and white has traditionally been used to signal clear legislative action. However, with many states now expanding their laws, we are exploring new ways to visually represent these developments without sacrificing clarity. Legislators are finding encouragement in the legislative successes of other states. Bills that pass in one state often pave the way for similar actions in others. By leveraging our experience from over 300 individual legislative efforts, we have been able to guide bill sponsors and drafters in avoiding early pitfalls and loopholes.

### John Deere Reaps What They Sow
Source: Repair.org
Published: 2025-01-21 15:19 UTC
URL: https://www.repair.org/blog/2025/1/21/john-deere-reaps-what-they-sow
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R3 · P4 · culture_probe

The recent news that the FTC and AGs from two farm states (IL and MN) have filed litigation against John Deere is a terrific step forward for Right to Repair laws already in effect or underway . Even the dissenting letters offered by two FTC commissioners support the Right to Repair but don’t like the timing of the filing, which is so close to a new administration in the White House. We understand the partisanship concerns, but there is no real excuse for a delay. John Deere has been dodging Right to Repair compliance for a decade in anticipation of actual enforcement being delayed or removed altogether. The FTC just laid down a marker that repair monopolies will be disbanded. Our organization, the Digital Right to Repair Coalition (aka repair.org) has been in the middle of this fight since our founding in 2013. We have worked tirelessly with legislators from both sides of the aisle to make sure that we can all fix our stuff. It’s our small “r” right to control the property we buy.
